data-go=\"266\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x057c03","title":"Better Ventilation for the Kingdom","volume":"婚后外传：The Liberty House Players","anchor":"chapter-0267","paragraphs":["The show ended with the pirate refusing the tyrant's crown. Mara had forgotten the last line, so she invented one. \"I prefer a ship with better ventilation.\" Luck gave Mara somewhat more prominence than her memory deserved.","Cassian nearly lost his own line laughing. Minna made the mistake useful by declaring the royal court a disgrace. The audience applauded what it assumed was a planned exchange. The cast accepted the honour without clarifying every accident.","Afterwards, they ate beneath the repaired stage awning. The flavor of the stew was Minna's secret, she announced, then immediately gave Noor the recipe. Her bosom glittered with the imitation jewels she would not remove until everyone had admired them. The old royalty had surrendered its domain to dinner, a realm in which practical appetite defeated theatrical misery.","Oliver spoke about his first years in the profession. Fame had promised freedom, then brought a dependency on applause he disliked admitting. His paralysis had altered his work profoundly, yet he refused a tidy account in which hardship automatically produced wisdom. Some days were dull. Some were excellent. Parenthood, friendship, and ordinary incompetence had taught him things too. His daughter kissed his cheek and told him the last category remained promising.","Minna rested her aching hands. Her arthritis and lameness did not need to disappear for the day to count as happy. She asked the waiter to cut her bread, then continued an argument about a costume. Esteem did not mean pretending she needed no assistance. Autonomy meant her preferences still directed it. Noor found the distinction so familiar that she did not turn it into a declaration.","Adela and Felix had brought their daughter to the table. The child asked what paternity meant, having heard the line three times. Felix answered simply that it concerned being a father. She considered this, then asked why the play's father needed a letter. Her parents agreed the script had not made that entirely reasonable. Parity in their household apparently included sharing criticism from a six-year-old.","The afternoon had not cured Adela's anxiety disorder or depression. It had given her work she enjoyed and a pause when she asked for one. She wanted to return for another scene next month. The request came from her, not a committee deciding she was ready. Readiness could be small without being false. Her insecurity had company now; it did not have command of the table.","The absent actor sent a message. Influenza remained unglamorous, flu was still a pest, and his fever had finally persuaded him that rest was more appealing than an encore. Minna held up the greeting-card for a photograph. The biscuits would be delivered without visitors. Nobody confused the old quarantine poster with a prescription for his current care. Fresh fashion might outdate a hat; it would not decide her fondness for it.","A former stage designer recalled the play's original political story. A realm in drought, famine threatened by deforestation, a child protecting the last tree: the company had once tried to compress every anxiety about civilisation into ninety minutes. No wonder the animation had collapsed. Its disintegration had felt like the end of their careers, but the props had outlived the financing.","\"We kept the good trees,\" Oliver said.","In the arena, the last stand-off ended with the self-deceiving king alone. His provocative talk of slavery could no longer hide his fear. The character's plight remained comic, while the story's necessity had shifted toward escape. No actor wanted the old repression back merely because it had once occupied the script. The revised scene had altogether changed its flavor.","\"Extinction narrowly averted,\" Minna replied.","The laughter was affectionate. They could name the old failure without living in its captivity. They had also dropped the original ending's demand for assimilation. The princess kept her language, and the pirate learned it. Their accord belonged to two people. It would not perpetuate a romance that offered one lover liberty at the cost of the other's voice.","A plurality of guests had responded to that change. One family said the citizenship scene mattered to them; another liked the jokes. The actors did not rank the reactions. The guild affiliation carried no compulsory reading of the play. Freedom included leaving with a different favorite moment. Noor liked the cause of the chaos best: Mara threatening a tyrant while trapped in her own veil. The resultant mess could wait until morning.","The ship's repair had left a clear record. The carpenter would return next week to finish the moisture protection. Hygiene supplies had been restocked, the loose fastener replaced, and the rash of last-minute requests finally subsided. A dearth of storage space remained. Minna called that an impossibility to be solved after pudding. Her crowded room had never been a persuasive reason to stop collecting hats.","Mara asked whether they wanted help with the boxes. \"No,\" Minna said. \"I want you to relax. There is a difference.\"","The refusal was kindly meant. Mara leaned back against Cassian, accepting a rare instruction with no desire to contest it. His hand settled at her shoulder. Their own relationship could still find a moment of emptiness and fill it with something ordinary, like a shared spoon.","The younger crew had learned to unload equipment from the repaired side, and their confidence began to take root. They did not need the old stars' repression or a hierarchy of fame. Their reasonableness made the work easier. Cassian proposed a toast to the living, the absent, and the formerly captive pirate.","Minna could conclude the evening without settling every old friction. The recession had cost the company dearly, and another scare would not make her grateful for hardship. Yet the little house remained traceable to their combined stubbornness. A muscular effort could move the remaining boxes tomorrow. Tonight, an out-of-date wig and an untrue story about a whale made her laugh too hard to stand.","\"No dishonor to the king?\" Mara asked.","\"He's full of stew.\"","The reply earned a laugh from Minna. The house's pervasive warmth was no delusion, but neither was it perfection. data-go=\"267\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x058c01","title":"The Missing Kettle","volume":"伙伴外传：The Picnic beneath the Summit","anchor":"chapter-0268","paragraphs":["Greta Hale wanted transcendence. Her father wanted a sandwich. Mara, watching their argument beside the mountain bus, proposed a mutually convenient compromise. Cassian had already bought extra bread. His self-interested reason was simple: a hungry wife became less affectionate.","The outdoor weekend marked Greta's first holiday after treatment for a tumour. She did not want to be introduced as a brave cancer survivor every five minutes. Her work, her terrible jokes, and her lifelong fascination with birds were equally available topics. Noor, an old friend, had promised a relaxing trip rather than a lecture. The promise became difficult when Greta's parents arrived with a large group of their neighbors. Neither parent was biologically related to her.","The proximate cause of the delay was a missing kettle. Greta's father, Soren, had treated retirement as a chance to become the standard-setter for picnic organization. Up until that morning, the kettle had occupied a permanent place in his pack. Its vacancy was a menace to civilized tea. His wife, Miri, suggested that he take steps to endure the hardship.","Greta had considered a solitary trip before unemployment changed her plans. The family's unity was affectionate and noisy, with turmoil over every missing object. Its travail could be comic. Her father's expressive face turned grim at a bump in the schedule; her mother promptly accused him of a fixation. Mara suspected the accusation contained affection, though she would not ask either to confess it.","Miri's cataract made small labels difficult to read. She had brought larger print, not a desire to be excluded. The family had already chosen a short route with the guide. Her husband asked about her glasses three times. \"Your golf handicap is a more urgent concern,\" she told him. He blushed and changed the subject. Miri's normality included a fascination with flowers and a dislike of official fuss.","Soren had once maintained equipment used by the Senate. The prestigious job had offered immunity from neither budget cuts nor a broken lunchbox. Its loss during an economic upheaval still colored his stories. The lodge's bankruptcy had also worried him, until its new owners explained their tie-up with the local school. The collaboration kept rooms used outside holiday season and gave students field-study access. It did not guarantee perpetual prosperity.","From the bus, the town's smog looked like a low gray sphere. On the ridge, clear air and a view of the wilderness made everyone suddenly quiet. Mara leaned against Cassian. The summit lay beyond the easy trail and was not their goal. They had come to walk together, not preserve an arbitrary idea of achievement. Greta's slight tan and smart jacket made her look ready for a photograph, but the story belonged to her.","A sore spot in her boot soon challenged that readiness. She stopped, changed to the shoes she had packed, and continued. Her vulnerability required a practical response, not the gloominess of everyone treating it as a warning of catastrophe. Miri offered a pinch of her own experience: never trust an attractive shoe on holiday. The humour did more to soothe her daughter than repeated questions about pain.","The guide described the valley's ecology. A former quarry had become a reserve after pollution damaged the stream. Recovery had taken years. A photograph of the old wreck of a processing shed stood beside one of the same slope now. The place's beauty was not proof that every damaged landscape healed unaided. Its traditional grazing rights had been retained in an agreed area. Local families and conservation workers had reached a consensus after a long quarrel.","Soren listened with surprising interest. He had expected a profitable resort brochure, not an account of work. His own complaint about modern stagnation began to sound too sweeping. A status quo could conceal a problem as easily as a change could create one. Miri declined to tell him she had said so. Her restraint was almost as impressive as his.","Mara's coat caught on a twig. A small graze appeared on her wrist. Cassian's attention was immediate and slightly ridiculous. \"A devastating wound,\" she said.","Soren's prestige had survived the loss of his old post. Its absence was a deficiency he felt keenly, but not a reason to despair. He had helped Miri out of a poverty trap; the guilt of fearing scarcity still lingered. His integrity remained intact through several indefensible attempts to direct everyone else's holiday. She loved him, and would not prolong one of his speeches.","\"To my peace.\"","His expression made her laugh. Greta accused them of prolonging the world's honeymoon. The accusation was fair, and Cassian accepted it without shame. He had no wish to let dignity become a handicap to happiness.","At a lookout, an out-of-state visitor recounted a nightmare journey delayed by a traffic tie-up. His pessimistic description ended with a sneeze so forceful that a bird fled. The guide offered him a tissue and moved the group on before the anecdote acquired a second chapter. The commonness of travel complaints transcended every border, Greta declared. A canal below was used to irrigate terraces, a sign told the general public.","Miri had read a book about neuroticism and announced that it explained her need to check a packed bag twice. Noor said one anecdote could not become a conclusive personality assessment. Miri agreed, then checked her bag again. The exchange made her laugh without making anyone else a diagnosis. A small family fixation could be funny when its owner was in on the joke.","The sunlight brought thirst. At the rest point, the guide asked everyone to drink from their own supplies and wait for the slower walkers. There was no urgency to force the next section. A relaxing morning could not be rescued by treating every pause as torment. Greta liked that arrangement. Her family had plenty of warmth; it sometimes needed help standing still. Distressed by a squeaking bag, he described it as a nightmare. Miri called his gloominess arbitrary and asked him to halt the commentary.","They reached a narrow bridge where loose boards had been marked for repair. An old sign suggested the route remained open. The guide halted the group and called the lodge. Miri stared at the gap. \"A time bomb of a sign,\" she said.","The alternative path was shorter and safe, the guide confirmed after checking. Soren's dismay at the altered plan lasted only until he saw a bench for lunch. The wilderness had not defeated them. It had simply rearranged the sandwiches."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1084,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0268\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">伙伴外传：The Picnic beneath the Summit</div><h1>The Missing Kettle</h1><p id=\"chapter-0268-p1\">Greta Hale <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9108\" data-words=\"9108\">wanted</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8626\" data-words=\"8626\">transcendence</a>. <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3891\" data-words=\"3891\">Her</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3165\" data-words=\"3165\">father</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-9108\" data-words=\"9108\">wanted</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7248\" data-words=\"7248\">sandwich</a>. kettle. It had been in the side pocket all along. The revelation produced a silence of pure gratification, followed by Miri's delighted laugh. \"A conclusive victory for my traditional method of checking twice.\" A bump in the bench threatened the cups. Cassian moved them, leaving the hazard unmentioned.","\"A standard-setter in vigilance,\" Greta said.","Noor tried to soothe the family quarrel by asking for tea. The plea worked. A brief turmoil about who had packed what dissolved into practical cooperation. Cassian brought the cups, Mara divided the bread, and Greta chose a solitary patch of sun with enough room for everyone beside her. The distinction made her grin.","The guide confirmed that the old bridge would remain closed. The lodge had to take steps to replace the contradictory sign, not merely hope every visitor saw the loose boards. The extent of the damage required an inspection before repairs. Soren's engineering experience did not give him permission to overrule the person responsible for the route. He recognized that without argument. Retirement had not deprived him of restraint. The conversation's coherence gave Greta more gratification than anyone could have planned.","There was no stagnation in the party now. Greta's tan deepened in the sun, and the walk renewed her thirst. A vacancy on the bench let her sit without asking anyone to move. The wilderness had plenty of room for the rest. Miri pinched her husband gently and told him not to turn a picnic into torment. His blush made Greta laugh.","Greta's cancer treatment had included removing the tumour. She would still attend follow-up appointments. Today she wanted to talk about something else. Mara asked about the bookshop she intended to reopen, and her whole face changed. Unemployment had made her anxious about money; illness had postponed the plan. A tourist season could help, though it would not offer immunity from rent or disappointing sales.","Her parents supported the idea but feared bankruptcy. Miri admitted her pessimistic habit came from a childhood poverty trap her own parents had barely escaped. It was not an excuse to veto Greta's choices. The older woman could remember hardship without making fear the standard-setter for every later decision. Her daughter took her hand. The little gesture did more than any ambitious declaration of unity.","Soren and Miri were not biologically related to Greta. They had adopted her as an infant, and the old neighborhood had objected to their different religious backgrounds, then their intermarriage, then the adoption. The family did not need outsiders to certify its coherence. Greta could list the jokes that belonged to each parent, and those were enough evidence for this picnic. She gave her father the larger sandwich.","Miri's cataract had made her mistrust a high viewpoint she once loved. The guide offered a lower path with the same lake in view. She chose it. Vulnerability need not turn every preference into a defeat. The outing's glory was not reserved for reaching the summit. A smart choice could preserve a relaxing afternoon without pretending anyone had become invincible.","A cheeky squirrel tested the bags. Cassian moved the food before it could graze their picnic. The animal's boldness made Greta laugh, and the spontaneity was rewarding enough that her father stopped looking at his watch. Her sore heel had settled after the shoe change; the hot spot would not be promoted into a family emergency. The proximate cause of his renewed worry was now a squeaking flask.","\"A time bomb,\" Miri pronounced.","\"It's tea,\" Soren said.","\"Then stop shaking it.\"","The out-of-state visitor returned, describing the town's smog as a plague upon tourism. He also blamed the traffic tie-up on everyone except his own departure time. Greta gave him a mild answer and offered him a cup. His complaint's predominance did not need to become the sphere of their whole afternoon. She could choose the conversation she wanted.","Noor watched that small authority with admiration. The old social liability of refusing a stranger's attention had not obliged Greta to endure an hour of it. Her integrity did not depend on smiling through discomfort. She could be kind without making herself a resting place for another person's grievance. It was a lesson Noor had sometimes needed herself. The guide asked them to stay out of the way of the maintenance crew.","The valley's ecology came back into view as they followed the lower path. The extent of the pollution cleanup was visible in paired photographs. Soren pointed to the processing wreck from the old display. The reserve had not emerged from nowhere; a local upheaval had made agreement possible. A consensus could follow conflict without claiming the former status quo had been harmless.","A large group of schoolchildren passed the lookout. They were distressed about a lost cap until its owner found it in her pocket. Their expressive relief resembled a small outbreak of hysteria. Soren's golf handicap would not survive such an audience, Miri said. His reply, a pinch of mock dignity, restored her good humor. The delay left them with an extra hour together.","Mara found a carved sphere beside a bench. Its plaque commemorated a former caretaker whose family had requested a quiet place rather than a grand statue. The stone's commonness pleased her. A dozen similar rocks lay along the path, yet this one carried a particular person's name. She thought of the people who had once helped her mother without expecting to become important.","Cassian noticed the change in her face. He did not interrupt. He simply stood beside her until she reached for his hand. The stillness held its own form of transcendence, entirely compatible with the ridiculous picnic they had left behind. His self-interested hope for her affection had become an ordinary willingness to wait.","A sneeze interrupted them. Greta apologized, blaming a loose feather from her scarf. The visitor launched into a grim description of local germs. Noor changed the subject before hysteria could acquire its own momentum. Nobody had asked for an improvised diagnosis. The feather drifted away, and the conversation followed a bird instead.","Up until the final turn, Soren still hoped to restore the original schedule. Then Miri asked him what he thought they had lost. He looked at his daughter, who was laughing at Mara's attempt to imitate a duck. His expression softened. The day's travail had not been wasted, and its happiness did not require a timetable.","They turned toward the lodge. The urgency had left their steps, and the forest no longer seemed like an examination."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1093,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0269\" Reach","volume":"伙伴外传：The Picnic beneath the Summit","anchor":"chapter-0270","paragraphs":["At the lodge, Greta took off her boots and declared the wilderness a qualified success. Her father asked which qualification she meant. \"It tried to ruin lunch. We prevailed.\" Cassian could discern her pleasure and found it rewarding. Their gratification pleased him. An arbitrary score would not.","The remark earned a cheer from Miri. The out-of-state visitor had finally found an audience for his traffic story elsewhere, and the family could relax without a running commentary. Mara sat beside Cassian at a wide window. The town's smog remained visible far below, while evening light turned the lake deep blue.","Greta's own tan made her grin look even brighter. The smart jacket had acquired mud on one elbow. A sore heel and a damaged schedule were not the whole day. Her vulnerability had come with choices she had been allowed to make. The family had not conquered a summit; they had managed a walk and a lunch without making one another miserable. She would take that version of glory. The extent of the day's good luck pleased her more than its small plague of delays.","The bridge sign had been removed. The lodge's manager promised to take steps to prevent a repeat, and the guide would check the revised route before recommending it. A time bomb of contradictory instructions had become a concrete task. No one was required to describe the morning as perfectly organized. The hotel could remain welcoming while admitting a repair.","Soren apologized for behaving as if every alteration were a threat. The proximate cause had seemed to be a missing kettle; the larger worry was his daughter's recovery and uncertain future. He had let that fear torment everyone else. She would halt his new fixation if necessary. The admission invited no gloominess. He asked Greta what kind of help she wanted with the bookshop.","Miri closed her book on neuroticism. Its small print was a menace, and she preferred the room's actual normality. There was no urgency to assess her own character. A vacancy by the piano invited her to sit. Soren, the standard-setter for imagined disasters, joined her without complaint. His renewed sense of prosperity included a full kettle. He would not let that pleasure decay into another lecture.","\"A shelf I can reach,\" she said.","He laughed, relieved to receive a task that did not encompass her whole life. His standard-setter's instincts could build something useful. They would discuss the design at home, where she could show him the room. He would not make the shop a liability by overspending. The plan gave unemployment a practical answer without pretending it solved all the finances. He had already retired once; she did not intend to make him a full-time unpaid caretaker.","Noor poured water for everyone's lingering thirst. Greta asked whether her bookshop could include an outdoor reading group. Her father began with the need for umbrellas, then stopped himself. \"Yes,\" he said. \"Tell me more.\" Mara left him with the last biscuit.","The answer made Mara smile. A family tie-up could produce another obligation or a little room for imagination. This one had begun to change its terms. Greta would keep a solitary hour for herself each day as well as time with the group. Its coherence did not require every person to feel the same thing at the same moment. Unity could survive that.","The tumour had altered her calendar, her work, and some of her confidence. It had not consumed the entire sphere of her life. She wanted a shop, poor jokes, a bird guide, and the right to dislike a sandwich. Greta disliked the predominance of treatment in every conversation. The room's lightness came from permitting that ordinary plurality. Noor thought it more valuable than an insistence that she be inspiring.","Miri's traditional fear of wasted effort had softened. The outing's travail had included one false alarm, a sore foot, and a borrowed pencil. Up until the first changed plan, she had expected the day to collapse. Now she remembered Cassian balancing cups and Greta calling a squirrel a thief. Her pessimistic forecast of a nightmare had been wrong in a welcome way.","His stories of the Senate could prolong a meal indefinitely. Tonight their prestige mattered less than the daughter's bookshop. The status quo of worrying aloud was not inevitable. A little stagnation in his retirement could be answered with a shelf. Miri's relief left her almost tearful; her husband looked at her with guilt, then offered the last orange. It was a mutually welcome settlement.","\"A relaxing disaster,\" she decided.","\"An indefensible phrase,\" Soren said.","\"I'm allowed one.\"","He kissed her cheek. The gesture had survived decades of intermarriage being treated as somebody else's business. Their family knew how to recognize its own happiness. Greta looked away with a smile rather than pretending to be embarrassed.","The lodge piano offered Mara another upheaval. Her fascination with its keys delighted Cassian. She could play a few tunes badly and several beginnings beautifully. Cassian requested one she knew all the way through. His theatrical despair at her deficiency of discipline failed to deter her. She chose a song from their wedding, and his expression changed before the second line. No photograph could have improved the memory she made of him then.","He sat beside her, turning a page she did not need. His self-interested reason for staying close was no longer disguised. She leaned against him on the final chord. A quiet sneeze from the audience threatened the solemnity, and everybody laughed. The moment survived the mock hysteria perfectly well. Greta had asked before she could activate the borrowed camera.","The lodge's new owners came to ask about the walk's turmoil. Greta praised the guide and mentioned the confusing sign. She left the old processing wreck and the wider politics of the reserve unmentioned; that was not the question being asked. Her answer could be useful without retelling every historical conflict in the valley. They thanked her, and promised a response about the route.","Outside, the light dimmed. The mountain wilderness became a dark shape against the sky, its summit no longer an invitation. Greta had found a form of transcendence with a bench in it. Mara had found an evening in which love could be as small as a hand holding a page. Cassian closed the music and offered her his arm.","They left the room together. Behind them, Soren began measuring an imaginary bookshelf with his fingers, and his daughter told him exactly where she wanted it."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1087,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\"
